Section 7.2: A Cosmic Chemical Formula

Here is a table showing the exact proportions of every atom, molecule, and mineral, to the same 4.9 ppm level. I know I put the nuclear mass at 1 gram. Its an atomic mass unit, but its useful for chemists who are used to the molar amounts. Anyhow, with that issue aside, one has a full inventory of the universe. These compounds are ubiquitous in deep space, inside the earth, and in comets, including the nebula that formed our solar system. Here it is.

Now that we have an idea of what things form from our cosmic soup of nuclei. It is time to turn out attention inwards again, back to the theory of everything that makes it all work.

Section 7.3: Atomic Structures and Substructures

As it turns out, every atom of the periodic table consists of a heavy and dense, positively charged nucleus, surrounded by two fields, the electronic field and the optic field. The discrete vibrations of the electronic field are the negatively charged lowmass electrons in atoms, while the discrete vibrations of the optic field are the photons of light, which carry the electric and magnetic forces from one place and time to another. Put together, electrons and photons create all of the solid state and chemical forces within any substance.

Within an atom, the electrons fill “shells” or orbitals two electrons at a time. Each of these orbitals is a standing wave of the electronic field. Since the nucleus is heavy and positively charged, it creates a sink for electrons to be attracted to. The reason why electrons fill one orbital at a time is because they are anti-symmetric under exchange. This is what makes an electron a particle of matter. For comparison, photons of light carry the electric and magnetic forces, because they are symmetric under exchange. Ultimately the reason why electrons are anti-symmetric, rather than symmetric, is tied to the fact that the electronic field itself only transforms half as much as the optic field does under a change in spacetime perspective. But enough about electrons and photons, what is the atomic nucleus anyways? After all, if electrons and photons were all there is, there would be only a single type of atom: Positronium and nothing else, and even then, given that Positronium atoms annihilate by themselves in less than a billionth of a second, what gives?

The answer lies in the fact that there are roughly 250 stable nuclei, and thousands more unstable nuclei. Once we zoom inside the nucleus, we understand that it is a chaos of nucleons and pions. It is said that nucleons are quark triplets, and pions are quark-antiquark pairs. But, in reality, the nucleus is a swarm of quarks and gluons, that are discrete waves in their respective fields, that only resonate fully as composite units. There is much to know as of yet about the field equations, such as their non linear behaviors, which are important to the structure and function of various nuclei, such as properties like radioactivity and binding energies. But all of the nuclei would not exist, without a chain of events of the early universe, known as the big bang.

Common Stable Particles:

For reference, here are the most common stable particles you will find in the universe at large. The table shows their relative abundance, and their properties like mass and spin. Since we are talking about nuclei with QED intact, we include the electric charge as well for completeness. Just know that this charge(like any other) doesn’t participate in the black hole duality. Here is the table:

*name:* *\# Abundance:* *Mass in MeV:* *Charge:* *Spin:*

alpha particles 1 3727.3794118 +2 e 0

protons 12 938.27208943 +1 e ½ hbar

electrons 14 0.51099895069 -1 e ½ hbar

neutrinos 18 \* 10^9 0 0 ½ hbar

photons 22 \* 10^9 0 0 1 hbar

Now we can see the exact properties of the common bulk matter and radiation that makes up our universe at large. Next up are the unstable particles.

Unstable Particles:

This table is the same as the last, but instead of an abundance, we have an average lifetime. These particles are either unstable constituents or unstable byproducts of those constituents or both. Here is the table.

*name:* *Lifetime:* *Mass:* *Charge:* *Spin:*

neutrons 880.2 s 939.56542194 MeV 0 ½ hbar

muons 2.197 us 105.6583755 MeV -1 e ½ hbar

pions 26 ns 139.57039 MeV +1 e 0

Then give that these particle are unstable, knowing what they decay into is of need. So here are the decay chains of these unstable particles.

*Decay Chain:*

neutron → proton + electron + electron-anti-neutrino

muons → electron + electron-anti-neutrino + muon-neutrino

pion → anti-muon + muon-neutrino

We can see that we already have defined the properties of the decay products too, so if you need to, like before, look back to any previous table. Next, are the consitutent particles that make up another composite particle.

Constituents:

Then for all composite particles, we note what it is made up of, its constituents, and not just what came out, but how much energy was required to break open the composite to reveal such a given constituent. And not only that, but the force field that mediates such a binding interaction. Here is the table for that.

*composite = its constituent parts:* *Mediator:* *Energy Debt:*

hydrogen molecule = 2 hydrogen atoms 2 photon fields -4.52 eV

helium atom = 1 helium ion + 1 electron 2 photon fields -24.6 eV

helium ion = 1 alpha particle + 1 electron 2 photon fields -54.4 eV

hydrogen atom = 1 proton + 1 electron 2 photon fields -13.6 eV

alpha particle = 2 protons + 2 neutrons 3 pion fields -28.3 MeV

proton = 2 up quarks + 1 down quark 16 gluon fields confined

neutron = 2 down quarks + 1 up quark 16 gluon fields confined

pion = 1 up quark + 1 down anti-quark 16 gluon fields confined

Now that we have an idea of what can form chemically when nuclei are included, to what these nuclei are made of and what they do, it is time to play an interesting thought game. What happens when we apply our theory of everything to reality itself. What will happen, given what we know? Lets find out…

Section 7.4: The Big Bang Theory of Cosmic Evolution

The big bang theory is not just a hypothesis like my black hole idea, and it isn’t a TV show either. This is the story of how everything came to be. What would genesis look like if it were run by my theory. As we shall see, extremely well. Lets now run through the gauntlet, and for the first time ever, find out how everything emerges out of nothing. Here we go!!!

The creation of everything

Before the beginning, there was absolutely nothing: No space, no time, no matter. And from nothing emerges everything. Thus, in the beginning, everything is an infinitely dense, infinitely hot, singular point of origins. This mysterious point, is what created everything that follows it, and obeys a set of rules known as the laws of physics.

Lets now pause for a moment and imagine. What kinds of things will emerge from the physics? How will fluids and chemistry come about from mathematics alone? In astronomy, how does order come from the chaos of waves? But for now, to be able to grasp everything that follows, we need to know a few things about physics.

The theory of everything is also known as the unified field theory, because that is what reality is made of: Waves! Put together, these waves described by the unified physics of Special Relativity, Universal Gravitation, and Quantum Mechanics, determine how the entire universe will be built. Now, to see its effect in action, lets turn our attention to the following sequence of events…

In the first tiniest fraction of an instant, the universe explodes into existence, as a rapidly expanding, tremendously hot and dense firestorm, spread uniformly across space, and that extends infinitely in all directions. It is like dropping a nuke in every cubic micron of space, but then to realize that your cubic micron is now a cubic kilometer, as space itself, ever more slowly, continues to expand out in all directions. The universe continues to expand and cool.

In just another few tiny fractions of an instant or so, the tremendously hot and dense, but uniformly spread firestorm that is infinite in extent, has expanded, cooled, and diluted, into a not so hot, not so dense firestorm, that continues to be uniformly spread, and infinite in extent. For some actual numbers, given that our “not so hot” universe, now means ten quadrillion degrees, imagine how incomprehensibly hot the universe was right at the start: ten quadrillion times hotter than ten quadrillion degrees! The universe continues to expand and cool.

So. With this in mind, lets now track the evolution of our particles. Every particle in existence, is constantly smashing into other particles. We say that the particles are in thermal equilibrium. The average kinetic energy per particle, is uniform throughout the cosmos. Looking at the sound waves within the aether, we can see the waves are vibrating with an extremely high frequency. We will measure frequency with a new system of units. The electron volt. Its written as, “eV”. Electron volts are useful because they can be understood as, energies, momenta, frequencies, and wavenumbers. In quantum field theory, everything is measured in electron volts. Light speed is 1 foot per nanosecond, and one electron volt of energy corresponds to a wavelength of 1.24 microns. As of now, the weak interaction and the electromagnetic interaction, are one and the same. Moreover, every particle is massless, and moves at the speed of light. This will change soon, when the temperature drops below 246 GeV. The universe continues to expand and cool.

The higgs boson activation

Passing 1 femtosecond, all of the quarks, antiquarks, leptons, antileptons, and the bosons, are almost in thermal equilibrium, and haven’t yet aquired mass from this so called higgs boson. Yet after 10 picoseconds, the temperature drops below the threshold of 246 GeV, and the higgs boson activates. The electromagnetic force carried by photons, is now distinct from the weak nuclear force, carried by the W plus, W minus, and Z bosons. These intermediate vector bosons, along with the higgs boson itself become very massive, on the order of 80 GeV, 92 GeV, and 125 GeV respectively. Since the electroweak bosons interact between themselves, we would expect to find confined states of these bosons. However, now that some of these electroweak bosons have a great deal of mass, removes any possibility of confinement. However these are not the only massive particles around. Every flavor of quark now has three possible masses. There are the up, charmed, and top flavors, and then the down, strange, and bottom flavors. The same applies to the charged leptons, which are the electron, the muon, and the tau. The neutral leptons, the neutrinos, do not have mass, and as such continue to move at light speed. The three top quarks, which are the heaviest flavor of the up type quarks, have masses of 173 GeV. That is much heavier than the W, Z, and the higgs boson. The universe continues to expand and cool.

After the next few hundred picoseconds, the top quarks and antiquarks, higgs bosons, Z bosons, and the W plus and minus bosons, are all gone. The energy of their annhililation, gets dumped into the remaining lighter particles. With these particles out of the picture, the weak interaction, reemerges as a short ranged force, 100 times shorter than a femtometer. In contrast, the quarks and gluons, are starting to feel their presence. This is why we call this the strong nuclear force. This force has litterally several metric tons of strength. The universe continues to expand and cool.

As we pass the ten nanosecond mark, the 4.5 GeV bottom quarks, 1.7 GeV tau leptons, and the 1.2 GeV charm quarks, annhililate away next. This seems like a losing battle, as all of the precious universe building particles, are just dissapearing, one by one, until nothing is left. After all of this, the only massive particles left are, the strange quarks, muons, down quarks, up quarks, and electrons, along with their antiparticles. So, to summarize. In these first instants, all sorts of exotic forms of energy and matter existed, such as quark matter, strange matter, and even antimatter, existed in great abundance. The universe continues to expand and cool.

The formation of primordial black holes

From the time that the Higgs boson activated up to now, which is roughly one microsecond or a millionth of a second after the beginning of everything, there is a tiny excess of matter particles over antimatter particles. And by tiny, I mean one extra matter particle, be it a quark, lepton, or even a boson, in a cosmic ocean of trillions possibly quadrillions of matter antimatter pairs, that are constantly being created and annhililated. The universe continues to expand and cool.

There shoudn’t be a difference at all. Zero, not one in anything, but zero. The fact that the universe went out of equilibrium at all is the question. How did the cosmic balance get violated? To answer this, we need to think about a series of steps that occurred throughout this time period. And it all begins when the annhililation rate exceeds the production rate of matter antimatter pairs, such as when the heaviest exotic particles annhililated away into less heavy particles, and so on. With only the lightest tier of particles present at this point in history, means most of the energy of the annhililation goes into heating the lighter particles by a given fraction. The universe continues to expand and cool.

As it turns out, this extra heat creates turbulence in the plasma of annihiliating particle antiparticle pairs. And this turbulence usually balances out given enough radiation pressure. But whenever we lose further particle species, their isn’t quite enough pressure for the plasma to even out. This means that there are moments in the history of our universe from 10 picoseconds onwards to 1 microsecond and beyond, that are going to have turbulence throughout. The universe continues to expand and cool.

When such turbulence persists under the constant struggle of gravity, small pockets of the quark gluon plasma collapse under their own weight, forming primordial black holes with the mass of a mountain at first. Then as the next tier of particles annihilate away, more turbulence is created, and plenty more prmordial black holes are created. These black holes have more mass, about that of a whole planet or large moon. But back then in the first microsecond, it was only a raging firestorm of equal amounts of matter and antimatter. The universe continues to expand and cool.

The creation of new black holes follows when the density overcomes the expansion rate of the observable universe. The observable universe wasn’t billions of light years across like it would be by the time this text you are reading was written, no. At 1 microsecond, the size of a universe that would be known would just be the time that information travels in that time. The fastest rate, which is the speed of light in a vacuum, makes the horizon of any observable part of the universe only 300 meters or 1000 feet in radius. Such a black hole would be the mass of a large planet to that of a small star in the modern universe. Inside such a contracting bubble, an event horizon forms, that makes the traversal one way, and a black hole is formed. In any case, the plasma inside appears to go through a big crunch, because there is no knowledge of continued expansionn outside the cosmological horizon. The universe continues to expand and cool.

But with such black holes forming all over the universe, creates huge explosions, at the scale of supernovas or larger, that create enough disequilibrium to force the imbalance between matter and antimatter. At this point in time, so far not much matter has been created from what seems like the vacuum. But the universe is not a vacuum, its a quark gluon plasma, that flows like the best motor oil, except its temperature is plummenting in from the quadrillions to trillions of degrees. These black holes where what created the first imbalance, that as we shall soon see, will form itself into all of the nuclear matter that will ever exist. The universe continues to expand and cool.

The quarks bind together into hadrons

Then, all of a sudden, right after 30 microseconds, the quarks and antiquarks bind together, into subatomic particles known as hadrons. Hadrons made of three matter quarks are known as baryons, and hadrons made of a quark antiquark pair are known as mesons. The lightest baryons are the nucleons, which have a mass of 940 MeV. The lightest mesons are the pions, which have a mass of 135 MeV. However the up quarks with masses of 2.01 MeV, and the down quarks with masses of 4.79 MeV, that make up these subatomic particles only contribute 1% of the total mass of the hadron. Strange quarks are the only exception to this rule, as they have a mass of approximately 95 MeV. These hadrons are confined, as it is impossible to remove any quarks or gluons for that matter, without creating other hadrons in the process. The fundamental strong nuclear force only acts within one of these subatomic particles, because of this confinement. This confinement is caused by the taffy life gluon flux tubes that emerge from the center of the hadron, and stretch out to the locations of the quarks. Again this is caused by the self interaction of the 8 types of gluons. Most hadrons have a physical size of around 1 femtometer. This is 100 times larger than the weak force can carry. In terms of electron volts, this corresponds to a value of 197 MeV. The temperature is dropping below 200 MeV. The universe continues to expand and cool.

Over the course of a few dozen microseconds after the quark gluon plasma transitions to a baryon meson gas, the resulting subatomic particles are much to heavy to exist, and as such they annhililate away. The small excess of matter quarks over antimatter quarks that are left behind, is now in the form of nucleons, which are the protons and neutrons. This is the very first real tangible matter in the universe. A proton is made of 2 up quarks and 1 down quark, and a neutron is made of 2 down quarks and 1 up quark. Yet these nucleons are outnumbered in the billions by the rediculous numbers of pions, that are being copiously produced in the seething 150 MeV heat. But what is important here is that as all kinds of quarks and all kinds of antimatter quarks, warred for supremacy, emerged a thin excess of protons and neutrons: The particles of matter we know today. The universe continues to expand and cool.

Yet the temperature not going to be this hot for long. So as the temperature drops below 135 MeV, pions begin to decay. The pions being made of quark antiquark pairs are very unstable. The charged pions are made of one up quark, and one anti down quark, or vise versa, and thus take a couple dozen nanoseconds to decay. The neutral pions on the other hand, are made of a quark antiquark pair of the same quark flavor, and so annhililate almost immidiately. Muons which weigh around 105 MeV, go next, after a couple microseconds. The universe continues to expand and cool.

And finally we reach the 100 microsecond mark in the history of the universe. If we were to take a census of all the particles in the universe as of now, there are 45 billion neutrinos, 20 billion photons, 15 billion electrons, 15 billion positrons, and finally 8 protons and 8 neutrons. There is also an excess of 8 electrons, that meet the requirement of the universe being electrically neutral. Like the W and Z bosons, the charged and neutral pions, act as short ranged force carriers. Since the pions are hundreds of times lighter than the electroweak bosons, they carry the residual nuclear force out to a 2.5 femtometers of distance, which is around three nucleons wide. However, it is still way too hot for the first nuclei to form. Yet. The universe continues to expand and cool.

The formation of primordial black holes creates our material universe

Once the quark gluon plasma has transitioned to a baryon meson gas, there was much more annihilation going on than ever before. And as we know, this leads to the formation of black holes. Since the observable part of the universe extends for about 2 miles, or 3 kilometers, means that the black holes that formed have that size, with a mass of about our home star, the sun, in the modern universe. But back in this moment when the plethora of Kaons, Lambdas, Omegas, all annhililated, there was so much turbulence, that not only did huge solar mass black holes form, but because of the massive amount of heat generated by the annihilations, pockets of the baryon meson gas, surrounding newly formed primordial black holes, returned back to the quark gluon plasma that existed prior. The universe continues to expand and cool.

When this plasma returns to the hadronic state once more, it triggers more annhililations, and thus more black holes. This feedback loop created the bulk of black holes that will ever exist! We call this substratum of primordial stellar mass black holes, dark matter, which as the modern universe matures, will form galaxies, stars, the whole shebang. But back in the first instants of creation, none of this structure exists, except for the matter particles that will form it. Right now the total abundance went from one in quadrillions of extra matter to one in a few billion of extra matter particles. The universe continues to expand and cool.

That is like saying we had a tiny amount, now we have a surplus of extra protons and neutrons, that will be enough to form the entire material budget of existence. Of the mass of dark matter to baryonic matter, 84% is invisible primodial black holes, and 16% is the thin excess of protons and neutrons! This will be slightly modified in a few hours to a few days from creation, but here is the dominant source. The universe continues to expand and cool.

From here on out, the black holes spread out as the universe expands, some black holes merge, releasing gravitational waves, but as the dominant form of matter has now arrived, the question is no longer, about what the imbalance is, but what will happen to this thin excess of baryons created in this apocalypse. The universe continues to expand and cool.

The matter antimatter annihilation

But going back to a fraction of a thousandth of a second since the beginning, for every proton or neutron created in this cosmic game of pinball, billions of photons, of gamma-ray light, were also created! The universe was dominated by energy, and yet again, what I referred to as a thin excess will amount to all of the matter in our universe that will ever exist! Along with the billions of gamma rays, there also exist billions of electrons and antimatter electrons, also known as positrons. The temperature is still too high for electron-positron pairs to begin annihilating, and they remain in flux. The universe continues to expand and cool.

Now we reach the 1 millisecond mark. The temperature is in the tens of MeV. Electrons and positrons also known as antielectrons, even the sea of photons and neutrinos, continue to bash into the few isolated protons and neutrons, turning them into one another. This happens due to the weak nuclear force that acts at a scale 100 times smaller than a proton or neutron. When an electron gets near an up quark inside a proton, of which the quark has an electric charge of +2/3. If it is close enough, the up quark will transmute into a down quark, with a charge of -1/3. The proton has turned into a neutron. If this neutron hits a positron, it will convert back into a proton. The massless neutrinos, that act exclusively with the short ranged weak force, also have the ability to convert protons and neutrons into eachother. So at this point in time, protons and neutrons are always converting back and forth. So at first, the firestorm of mostly gamma radiation and electron-positron pairs, contains an equal number of protons and neutrons. The universe continues to expand and cool.

However the neutron is only 780 keV heavier than a proton, and the electrons weigh in at a tiny 511 keV. So as the temperature drops to a few MeV, it becomes much easier to make protons than to make neutrons in these conversions, and so there are fewer and fewer neutrons around as compared to protons. Over the next second or so, electrons and positrons are also starting to go through the same annihilation, that the quarks went through right at the start of creation. Then, as we pass the 1.8 second mark, the electron positron pairs begin annhililating, and over the next few hours, the concept of antimatter will become a distant memory. But now, with a universe 3 seconds old, some lower energy electrons and positrons still exist. The universe continues to expand and cool.

Also around this time, the neutrinos have not annhililated with their antineutrinos, because of their almost massless nature. Yet, as the universe continues expanding as it always does, the neutrinos and antineutrinos are having great difficulty turning protons and neutrons into one another. This is because of the extremely short range of the weak force. When this process begins, the ratio of number of neutrons to number of protons, was dropping dramatically for a few seconds. The universe continues to expand and cool.

So now that neutrinos cannot change the number of protons or neutrons, the declining neutron number then settles at a value of 3 neutrons per 13 protons. Yet, neutrons are ever so slightly heavier than protons, and should decay. As of now the lifetime of a neutron is around 880 seconds, which is too far into the future as of now. The universe continues to expand and cool.

One thing to note is that when the majority of neutrinos stopped interconverting the protons and neutrons, there were still quite a few higher energy neutrinos in the distribution, still in equilibrium with the remaining electrons, positrons, and photons. This means that for a few more seconds, these neutrino outliers are still interconverting the protons and neutrons between one another, so while the number 3 neutrons per 13 protons seems like it should be taken at face value, it isn’t set in stone. It is a complicated mixture of particles that interacts based on the temperature and concentration of a given sub-species of particles after decoupling. This is the way things are for most gradual proccesses. However after 10 seconds, this cosmic neutrinos background will be essentially non-interacting with the rest of matter, except through the gentle tug of gravity, which “sees” all forms of energy and momentum present, but not their structural content. The universe continues to expand and cool.

The formation of galactic black holes

After a second since everything began, more and more massive black holes have been forming. With a radius of over 1 light second, any black holes formed from here on out, will have masses of over a 100 thousand suns. Compare this to the first few microseconds, when black holes produced the dominant forms of matter in our universe, But since the universe has been expanding and cooling, the density of matter and radiation is too thin for more matter production, despite the fact that there are billions of electron positron pairs to yet annhililate. The universe continues to expand and cool.

So, black holes are still forming, just at a slower rate. However, they are not producing much matter at this point from the annhililating electrons and positrons. Trace to insignificant amounts were produced. At this point, the black holes are supermassive when they from. Supermassive’s are the seeds for the later formation of galaxies. So to summarize galaxies required all three stages of primordial black holes to form. The universe continues to expand and cool.

Why? The dark matter that will hold the galaxies together into the cosmic web, is already forming, and being that it is the combined gravity of all black holes, microscopic, stellar, and supermassive, without them structure wouldn’t exist. Not to mention that stellar mass primordial black holes formed the excess of matter over antimatter, and now constitute the bulk of dark matter as of now. Even the supermassive’s are what is needed to create the galaxies themselves. The universe continues to expand and cool.

So to summarize a galaxy is made of both dark matter and normal baryonic matter, that formed in the first microsecond, and needs the supermassives to spark them into life. Without any stage of matter or black hole formation, galaxy formation would be impossible! The universe continues to expand and cool.

As soon as the supermassive black holes have formed, all three stages of black holes begin interacting with one another through gravity. The gravitational waves produced from the mutual gravity of passing black holes stirs the plasma ever so slightly. Also the black holes are immune to the radiation pressure, so they’re structure immediately roughens to form a cosmic scaffolding of purely black hole dark matter. The universe continues to expand and cool.

The nuclear fusion into alpha particles

One second passes, then five, then 20 seconds have elapsed. Then a minute passes. The residual nuclear force, is strong enough to bind the nucleons together, but this isn’t happening at all. This is because when a proton and neutron collide and form a deuteron, a high energy photon will break the first nuclei right back down to the nucleons that already exist. But a deuteron has a binding energy of 2 MeV, so it should be stable enough to exist. Nope. Remember that there exist 20 billion photons. Even if the average temperature of this thermal radiation is on the order of 100 keV, or 0.1 MeV, there will still be a few outliers with energies over 2 MeV. Even if a few very high energy photons exist, its over for a deuteron. The universe continues to expand and cool.

Yet again, as always, the universe expands and cools. Also, neutrons decay, so given that we started with 3 neutrons at first, after 3 minutes, there are only 2 neutrons left, as one of the neutrons has beta decayed back to a proton, along with an electron and an antineutrino. We now have 20 billion gamma ray photons, along with 14 protons and 2 neutrons. Also, an excess of 14 electrons also exist, along with the hundreds of millions of electron-positron pairs that are under constant annhililation. The universe continues to expand and cool.

So this means that for the next four minutes or so, not much changes to the firestorm of gamma-rays and annihilating electron-positron pairs. A few of the many neutrons that still exist, have naturally decayed back into protons, so while the neutron to proton ratio used to be 1 in 5 a few minutes ago, right now its closer to 1 in 7. The average lifetime of a free neutron is 880 seconds, otherwise put, 14 minutes and 40 seconds. I should note that although the temperature is well high enough for fusion to occur, the sheer energy and number of gamma ray photons will shred down any nuclei that try to form, so the universe, as of now, contains only free protons and free neutrons. The universe continues to expand and cool.

Then, right after the clock strikes 4 minutes and 20 seconds, does something dramatic happen: Nuclear fusion! Now, as the temperature decreases, there are less and less photons with energies of 2 MeV, and when the temperature, or average photon energy, drops below 78 keV, deuterons are stable, and the fusion process continues on. In other words, the intensity of gamma radiation is now low enough for the first nuclei to not be blown apart. Most nuclei that could form at this point, like tritons or helions, also have binding energies not much more than deuterons. However there is one special kind of nucleus, known as an alpha particle. It has a whopping binding energy of 28.3 MeV. That is to say 28,300 keV! The universe continues to expand and cool.

At this point the majority of the nucleon excess is in protons, alpha particles, and neutrons. But if one zooms out to get a larger sample size, not of 16 nucleons, but of 10 million nucleons, one finds out that not only do other nuclei form, but their abundance shows they act as intermediate steps from the conversion of single nucleons into alpha particles. Out of 10 million nucleons, the peak number of nuclei is the following: 5,041 deuterons, and 683 tritons. A deuteron consists of a bound state of one proton and one neutron. A triton consists of a bound state of one proton and two neutrons. After this peak in abundance, the deuterons and tritons get used up in the formation of more alpha particles. The universe continues to expand and cool.

An alpha particle consists of two protons and two neutrons. The stability of alpha particles, is extremely important in the near future of the nuclear reactions. Every proton and every neutron in existence are now smashing together to build alpha particles: Clusters of 2 and 2, that is to say, 2 neutrons with 2 protons. The universe is now 265 seconds, or 4 minutes and 25 seconds old. At this point, as soon as alpha particles form, it becomes extremely difficult to destroy them. All nuclei that contain any neutrons, including the soon to decay neutrons themselves eventually get locked up inside these alpha particles. The universe continues to expand and cool.

As the number of free neutrons declines to zero, the nuclear reactions which have been proceeding rapidly, radically changes its form. Out of 10 million nucleons total, the following abundances have occurred as follows: The number of deuterons drops from 5,041 deuterons to 264 deuterons. The number of helions grows from zero to 102 helions. A helion consists of a bound state of two protons and one neutron. Also, the number of tritons drops from 683 tritons to 1 triton nucleus. This it will remain for years to come. The universe continues to expand and cool.

For the next 10 seconds, the number of alpha particles, grows to a value of 1 alpha particle for every 12 protons. The story of alpha particles, is not over yet. As the clock reaches 4 minutes and 30 seconds, all of the neutrons get used up in the reaction, and the 10 seconds of nuclear fusion grinds to an immediate halt. Why so early? Even though fusion is still occurring in vast quantities, not much happens to the composition of the nuclei. The 12 protons, and 1 alpha particle, are all smashing into one another. When such a collision takes place, it produces an unstable nucleus with 5 or 8 nucleons. Such a nucleus will undergo alpha decay, and revert right back to the same nuclei as before. The universe continues to expand and cool.

Also around this time, from 5 to 20 minutes after the big bang, there is still plenty of fusion occuring, but just because heavier elements are improbable doesn’t mean they do not form. So to be fair, I will list out the trace nuclei that do form around this time. We talked about 12 protons and 1 alpha particle, that dominates the nuclear budget. Then we expanded our inventory from 16 nucleons up to 10 million, and found other nuclei, namely 264 deuterons, 102 helions, and 1 triton. Tritium has a lifetime of 12.32 years which is too far into the future as of now. But out of 1 quadrillion nucleons or 15 powers of ten less than hydrogen and helium totals to, there are the beryllium and lithium isotopes. In order of declining abundance, we have the unstable beryllium-7 nuclei with a lifetime of 53.22 days, that are currently decaying into lithium-7 nuclei. Currently Beryllium-7 is at an abundance of one nucleus in 500 million nuclei total. In the first 5 to 7 minutes after the beginning, the abundances of Lithium-7 peaked at 1 nucleus in 20 million nuclei total. But after 10 minutes, there was still plenty of nuclear reactions that destroyed Lithium-7 so it dropped to a value of 1 nucleus in 3 billion nuclei total, with Beryllium-7 now in the lead of trace isotopes. Then there is Lithium-6, with an abundance of 1 nucleus in 1 trillion nuclei total, making it the rarest of trace isotopes. The content of the nuclei are the following: Beryllium-7 has 4 protons and 3 neutrons, Lithium-7 has 3 protons and 4 neutrons, and Lithium-6 has 3 protons and 3 neutrons. All of these trace nuclei are easy to destroy and hard to make, hence the very low abundances. The universe continues to expand and cool.

Strangely however, free neutrons, which were common before the universe was 4 minutes old, are now not completely gone! Yes it is true that the alpha particles contain neutrons, so when that majority forms and stabilizes, the number of free neutrons declined rapidly. But now, 5 to 20 minutes after creation, the neutron number doesn’t quite settle, not because of nuclear reactions, but because all neutrons have an average lifetime of 880 seconds which is 14 minutes and 40 seconds. So although not zero, it is still dropping. At 8 minutes, there is one free neutron per 35 million nuclei total. So while protons and alpha particles domainate the nuclear matter budget, neutrons are one of the trace nuclei, but are extremely radioactive and after 1 hour or so, will be essentially all gone, converted back to stay as protons one last time. The universe continues to expand and cool.

After some more time for the universe to expand and cool, the universe ages past the 20 minute mark. However, these stable nuclei are positively charged, and like charges repel. This means that there is an electric potential barrier right beyond the reach of the residual nuclear force. As the nuclei slow down, there is less and less kinetic energy available, and by the end of the first hour of the universe, almost every nucleus avoids every other nucleus around it. The ever expanding and cooling universe now has a chemical formula of 12 protons, 1 alpha particle, which goes along with an excess of 14 electrons over millions of electron-positron pairs, and also the 22 billion photons of gamma-ray light. The universe continues to expand and cool. The fusion process halts completely, as the 12 protons, and 1 alpha particle, not only fail to create new nuclei, but at this point they don’t touch eachother in the first place. The nuclei just avoid eachother from now on. The universe continues to expand and cool.

In the background, the invisible intangible radiation of the 18 billion neutrinos has been existing alongside the 22 billion gamma ray photons. Since neutrinos decoupled within the first second of existence, they’ve been hardly interacting with anything. But that doesn’t mean they do not exist. As electron-positron pairs annhililate away into photons, this increases the photon temperature, but not the neutrino temperature. And since everything coupled to the photon temperature as of now, its useful to ignore the lower but original neutrino temperature. If you must know, compared to a photon temperature of 1, the neutrino temperature is the cube root of 4/11. The universe continues to expand and cool.

Mentioning the average temperature is fine if everything were in perfect thermal equalibrium. Almost so, but the fusion of protons and neutrons into alpha particles produced plenty of high energy gamma rays, on the order of 1 to 10 MeV. Since fusion has already occurred and halted, the average mjority photon temperature is much lower. It will take a long time for these high energy photons to equilibriate with the other photons to reform the perfect thermal distribution. Compared to neutrinos in the first seconds, they also have high energy outliers, but neutrinos don’t interact with matter, so those outliers still exist, stretched out by the expansion of space itself. But we should expect that after a few months the equilibrium of photons, which do interact with matter such as electrons and nuclei, will eventually equilibriate once again. The universe continues to expand and cool.

Hours into the creation of the universe, the last few dozen electron-positron pairs finally annihilate away into even more photons of gamma-ray light. The particle count is now, 12 protons, 1 alpha particle, 14 electrons, and 22 billion photons. This it will remain for almost an eternity. As a sense of comparison, a proton weighs as much as 1,836 electrons, and an alpha particle weighs as much as 7,294 electrons. The universe continues to expand and cool.

The black holes arrange into a cosmic web

At this point, we are hours into the formation of everything. No more annhilations are happening. By remembering that the universe is radiation dominated, means a few things. First, there are no more black holes that are to form any more. The past annhililations were purely thermal in nature, and are classified as radiation. With only the ghostly neutrinos and the photons of gamma ray and X ray light dominating the energy budget for radiation, no annhililations are occuring, and as such the formation of primordial black holes has stopped. The universe continues to expand and cool.

The second effect of the radiation dominating, is that its pressure holds up normal baryonic matter from collasping under it’s own gravity. And also, it’s the photons of light doing the work of keeping the turbulence down to a minimum, as the neutrinos hardly interact with anything. From now on, this primordial plasma, of protons, alpha particles, electrons, and photons, are all scattering off one another hard enough to keep the plasma uniform to 1 part in 100 thousand. This it will remain for a very long time. The universe continues to expand and cool.

However, the primordial black holes that already exist don’t follow this same logic. They continue to suck in everything nearby, including other black holes. Even though no new black holes have formed, the 84% of dark matter continue to exist. With the other 16% of normal matter locked up with the radiation, it is this 84% that will build the cosmic web. So although, primordial black holes and normal matter seem to be running the show, weight for weight, its the radiation energy that has dominated and will continue to domninate for a very long time, possibly for the next few to dozen millennia into the future. The universe continues to expand and cool.

But that doesn’t stop the primordial black holes from pulling on one another by their own self gravity. Black holes orbit and merge, releasing gravitational waves. Soon, we have a cosmic web of dark matter, that although insignificant at this moment, will play a key role later on in the history of everything. The universe continues to expand and cool.

Remember those trace nuclei, like beryllium-7, lithium-7, lithium-6, and so on. They are easy to destroy. These primordial black holes also had a huge impact on the destruction of these low binding energy nuclei. Unlike the alpha particle which is a nuclear brick. The trace nuclei are like nuclear fluff. So when this radioactive nuclear mixture nears a black hole, the black holes also grow from consuming anything that cross their event horzons: all forms of radiation, all forms of matter, even other black holes. This heats the surrounding matter up in the immidiate vicinity of their accretion disks. This heat then destroys many trace nuclei in the region. This is why the total number of all trace nuclei are dropping. It isn’t radioactivity. Only neutrons, which are non-existent, Beryllium-7 which is next to go, and Tritium which is to go after that. But radioactivity is passive, it is disctinct from the active fission of nuclei by high energy photons of radiation. So depending on the stability, and closeness to these hot spots, many nuclei are disintregated. This changes the abundaces of many trace isotopes in the early universe. But as time marches on, most of the accretion dies down, due to matter and radiation thinning out. The universe continues to expand and cool.

Also these hot spots are creating even more high energy photons, that while many disintegrate nuclei, the scattered light still has much to high energy, so along with the nuclear reactions that occurred from 3 to 20 minutes after the big bang, the combined effect is to make it very difficult for the photons to reach equilibrium again. But as time passes, the universe expands. The expansion of space stretches all the field quanta out, and this lowers the temperature of the universe, coupled or uncoupled. With all this time on our hands, the black holes have spread out enough to make the photons begin to reach equilibrium. But the completion of this proccess into pure random heat will take many months. The universe continues to expand and cool.

The primordial plasma clears up

This whole time, electrons and photons, pinball one another. The scattering of, electrons and photons, is fully described by the theory of QED. QED begins with the spacetime of special relativity. This spacetime is described by the O(3,1) group. The particles of QED, are representations of this symmetry group. Electrons are massive charged spinors. Photons are massless neutral vectors. Put together, they interact via a minimally coupled U(1) internal symmetry group. As of now, QED was not the main player in the history of the universe, as it was unified with other internal symmetries, into the SU(5) group. The universe continues to expand and cool.

However, now that the nuclear reactions have stopped, QED is what is left over. The electrons and photons form a plasma known as the cosmic soup. In it, electrons are known as cathode rays, and photons are known as X rays. The chemistry of the universe is pretty much constant after the first few hours have elapsed. The cosmic soup clocks in at a temperature of 1 keV. Otherwise put, 11.6 million degrees kelvin, and dropping. In fact, it’s still too hot for anything tangible to exist at all. The universe continues to expand and cool.

A day passes, then a week goes on by. The only thing that changes is the expansion of the universe. Nothing has happened to the plasma. Even after a whole month of this expanding and cooling, the temperature, pressure, and density, are all surely dropping, yet nothing else has happened to the cosmic soup. Why? As compared to the nuclei, which are femtometers in size, and weigh in at many GeV, electrons are nothing in comparison. Even the photons that carry the electromagnetic force, is 137 times weaker than the other forces. So not only do the electrons need to slow down, they need to be captured by the nuclei, for the first atoms to form. The universe continues to expand and cool.

Every electron, cannot be crammed into a volume smaller than 386 femtometers, without producing antimatter. This is assumming that the cathode rays are moving near light speed. But, at this day and age, the cathode rays have much longer wavelengths than this, since they’re moving much slower than light speed. The math of QED, says that the electrons must be moving 137 times slower than light speed for them to be captured. With the electrons so spread out compared to the nuclei already, the cathode rays must have wavelengths hundreds of thousands of times longer than the nuclei are big. And that is a big problem. The universe continues to expand and cool.

As weeks pass by, something is happening to the rarest of trace nuclei, as the beryllium-7 nuclei with a lifetime of 53.22 days are gradually decaying into lithium-7 nuclei, increasing the number close, but not quite to the value it had when the universe was only 5 minutes old. All of this is only significant at scales with an gigantic sample size of over 1 billion nuclei total. But in the grand scheme of things, nothing has changed to the chemistry of the uniform cosmic ocean of fire. The universe continues of expand and cool.

As months turn into years, then into decades, the gamma ray photons have stretched out so much, they are now X-ray photons. As the firestorm of a universe continues to expand and cool, not much happens for a very long time. The universe as a firestorm that is uniformly spread and infinite in extent, continues to expand and cool. However, if one looks at our huge sample size of 10 million baryons, something did just happen. Our last triton nucleus has beta decayed into one more helion nucleus. So while 102 helions used to exist, the extra helion added in, gives us 103 helions. This is the last time a beta decay happens for a very long time. The neutron inside the triton nucleus took 12.32 years to decay, compared to a free neutron decaying in 880 seconds, its an eternity. The universe continues to expand and cool.

After all of this radioactivity, nuclear fusion, black hole production and growth, the photons of light are slightly off equilibrium, and now the universe has produced enough of a disturbance of one part in 100 thousand. Now after many years, the composition and energy distribution of the matter and radiation has reached equilibrium once more. But instead of this heating just spreading out, it rings like a bell, or better yet, like an expanding balloon, vibrating once every few years or so. The universe continues to expand and cool.

Decades pass by, then centuries, and eventually millennia pass by in the hundreds. The universe is so much more diluted now that its self gravity, which is slowing down the expansion of the universe, takes more time to expand by the same amount as usual. Up to this point, the temperature-time relation has been following an inverse square root curve. At one second the temperature was 1.27 MeV, so at 100 seconds or 1 minute, 40 seconds, the temperature drops ten fold to 127 keV. To arrive at the temperature we take the time to an exponent of negative one half. This pattern is about to break, as the exponent will shift to a value of negative two thirds. If the temperature in the near future of 100 thousand years is 620 meV, then if we project out to 1,000 times into the future of 100 million years, the temperature will drop a hundred fold to 6.2 meV. This is because matter will soon be dominating the expansion history of the universe. The universe continues to expand and cool.

By matter I mean all forms of mass including not just nuclei and electrons, but also primordial black holes that outweigh all of our baryons by a factor of 5. But that is for a later chapter. For now also realize that its not just the photons that constitute radiation, but also the ghostly neutrinos. If we just looked at the total energy budget of the universe, around this time the temperature has finally dropped so low to the point that our small excess of matter over antimatter now dominates its gravitational evolution. The universe continues to expand and cool.

Moreover, all of the photons of X-ray light, have stretched out into photons of Ultraviolet light. However, as the universe expands, a new problem surfaces. Billions of photons might have an average energy of 5 eV or 58 thousand degrees kelvin, yet the truth is that as always there are high energy outliers. Some photons have huge energies well over 269.3 eV and as such we will have to wait until these high energy photons are gone until atoms can fully form. The universe continues to expand and cool.

Out of the total of 10 million nucleons the following happens: As the universe expands and cools below 16 thousand degrees, each and every of the 625,000 alpha particles captures an electron forming 625,000 helium-4 ions. Of the far and few between helions, each of the 103 helion nuclei also capture an electron, forming 103 helium-3 ions. The universe continues to expand and cool.

Next, as the universe expands and cools below 7 thousand degrees, each and every of the 625,000 helium-4 ions capture another electron, this time forming 625,000 helium-4 atoms. Again, the same thing occurs for 103 helium-3 ions, as they too capture their second electrons, forming 103 helium-3 atoms. The universe continues to expand and cool.

Then, as the universe expands and cools below 4 thousand degrees, each and every of the 7,500,000 protons captures an electron, forming 7,500,000 hydrogen-1 atoms. Of the far and few between deuterons, each of the 264 deuteron nuclei also each capture an electron, forming 264 hydrogen-2 atoms. The universe continues to expand and cool.

After that, as the universe expands and cools below 3 thousand degrees, the number of free protons and free electrons have not fully recombined into hydrogen-1 atoms. Instead, 578 of the 7.5 million free protons and with them 578 free electrons also remain. The last 578 free electrons have for the first time in history, stopped scattering light, leaving the now visible light photons to begin streaming freely throughout the cosmos. Let There Be Light!

The cosmic dark ages

The universe continues to expand and cool. 377 millennia have elapsed since the beginning, and is flooded with atomic hydrogen-1 and atomic helium-4. The color temperature of the radiation is pushing 3 thousand degrees Kelvin, but is gradually dropping as we speak. Of the 10 million nucleon total. We have the following abundances for baryonic matter: 7.5 million hydrogen-1 atoms, 625 thousand helium-4 atoms, 578 free protons, 578 free electrons, 264 hydrogen-2 atoms, 103 helium-3 atoms. However it remains to be seen what kinds of chemistry will occur in this white hot, near vacuum atmosphere. The universe continues to expand and cool.

From 3,000 degrees kelvin, or roughly 5,000 degrees Fahrenheit, the radiation temperature and matter temperature though decoupled, are dropping in tandem as we speak. As the temperature drops below 1,150 degrees kelvin, or 1,610 degrees Fahrenheit, of the 10 million nucleon total, 4 hydrogen-1 atoms stick together in pairs, forming 2 hydrogen-1 molecules. The catalyst here is the rare but intermediate formation of the hydrogen molecular cation, formed of two protons held together by a single electron. It’s the result of the collision between a hydrogen-1 atom, with a free proton. The universe continues to expand and cool.

Then, as the radiation temperature drops below 530 degrees kelvin, or a relatively hot 490 degrees Fahrenheit, the matter temperature begins cooling twice as fast. Instead of the radiation temperature which as a time-temperature exponent of negative two thirds, the matter temperature now has a time-temperature exponent of negative four thirds, which means that if the radiation temperature drops from 530 kelvin to a bone cold 53 kelvin, the matter temperature will have dropped to 5.3 kelvin above absolute zero, twice as cold as the radiation temperature has dropped. The universe continues to expand and cool.

Next, as the radiation temperature drops below 300 degrees kelvin, or a luke warm 80 degrees Fahrenheit, another 34 hydrogen-1 atoms have stuck together in pairs, forming an additional 17 hydrogen-1 molecules, making a total of 19 hydrogen-1 molecules. This time the catalyst here is different. It is the rare but intermediate formation of the hydrogen anion, formed of a single proton holding two electrons. This time, it is the result of the collision between a hydrogen-1 atom, with a free electron. The universe continues to expand and cool.

The 19 hydrogen-1 molecules are all each formed of two protons held together by two electrons. Below room temperature, the following census holds in detail: 7,500,000 hydrogen-1 atoms, 625,000 helium-4 atoms, 578 free protons, 578 free electrons, 264 hydrogen-2 atoms, 103 helium-3 atoms, and our 19 hydrogen-1 molecules. The universe continues to expand and cool.

So far, we have been talking about only a small fraction of what matter and energy exists out there. It is like saying we have a jar filled with 100 grams of gas. As we know from earlier, 75 grams would be atomic hydrogen-1, and 25 grams would be atomic helium-4. The truth that has been overlooked is this conundrum: An additional 530 grams of invisible intangible matter also exists.

This form of matter consists of countless primordial black holes. A black hole is a region of space with a gravitational pull that is so strong that not even light, the fastest thing in the universe, cannot escape from its deadly grip. This is an extremely important concept: All fundamental particles are rotating black holes with the same mass and same spin as the wave they constitute. This means that all black holes are fundamental waves too in the same sense as of any other wave, such as the electrons of electricity and the photons of light are all fundamental waves. This means that we should widen our search for extremely exotic wave types that hardly interact with anything. These primordial black holes will be a fundamental turning point in the evolution of what follows.

The fate of the universe rests on the unification of physical law. There are three main sets of new phenomena, that were incorporated into the three pillars of modern science. The three pillars are: special relativity, universal gravitation, and quantum mechanics. The associated physics reduces to our common sense, by making a series of approximations. So even though our common sense worldview works well in the everyday, in the grand scheme of things, our universe is stranger than fiction.

With this in mind, a black hole is simply a natural consequence of these three main concepts of physics. In a sense, every black hole is just as fundamental as every other particle of matter and light that exists. In fact, the only difference is their mass and spin. Therefore, all rotating black holes are just as “real” as anything else that exists. Lets see what has happened to this mysterious form of matter, so that we can continue our story of everything!

All of these black holes have been colliding and merging, since the beginning, forming a sprawling network of black holes, called the cosmic web. The cosmic web is truly massive. But the reason I bring this up now is because, before the universe was 377 thousand years old, the radiation pressure was holding up the ordinary matter from gravitational collapse. But now, going back to when the light has been set free, there is no more radiation pressure to prevent collapse, and collapse it goes. The universe continues to expand and cool.

As the universe expands and cools, over the next few million years, the color temperature dropped from white, to pale yellow, to vivid orange, to cherry red, and finally fades into the near infrared. If we were somehow around back then, we could feel these rays as radiant heat, although there would be nothing to see. Just a uniform still darkness all around. The universe continues to expand and cool.

As for the matter, the gas is no longer uniform, and as the contrast increases, the gravity of both the black holes and the atoms of hydrogen and helium, becomes unstoppable. In just a few hundred million years, the cosmic web of gas starts resembling the cosmic web of black holes. This is like the scaffolding of a building. The black holes are the scaffolding, and the gas of normal matter is following the same pattern. The universe continues to expand and cool.

As this new consolidated cosmic web continues to expand and grow, the scene will soon change dramatically to include new structures never seen before! The cosmos is still cold and darkness envelops the night sky. But a chain of events will form a hierarchy of scale, where everything will eventually depend on everything else. The universe continues to expand and cool.

But before the sky lights up, the darkness has one last secret. Of the rarest nuclei that exist, atoms of lithium stick together in rows and columns to form dust grains, and are very rare. Each atom is one in trillions now, but the last time we counted the census it was rounded to 1 part in 16, 1 part in 10 million, and 1 part in a quadrillion. Within this last quadrillionth of matter, rare isotopes like carbon, nitrogen, oxygen, and many others, have been created in the first hour, but are so rare to not contribute a signifcant difference. Could it be that life on earth arose, because there was a random surplus of these nuclei? Everywhere else, there must have been negligible amounts, and as such we will only find life where the materials will be concentrated. This will soon change as the first stars form, within galaxies: Places that stars live out their lives and die. The universe continues to expand and cool.

The formation and evolution of cosmic structure

The universe is now 300 million years old, and as the cosmic web continues to expand and grow, the first irregular galaxies form along the filaments of the cosmic web. Just like dew drops on a spider’s silk, each of these galaxies is a loose collection of stars, gas, dust. As time marches on in the billions of years, the irregular galaxies merge into larger irregular galaxies. As it turns out, the densest regions of the cosmic web are where the irregular galaxies form. But since the cosmic web grows, the galaxies are carried along with the filaments of the invisible matter that consitutes most of the cosmic web. Whenever the filaments of the cosmic web lock together, is when most galaxies cluster and merge.

Zooming inside one of these irregular galaxies, there is plenty of gas from the big bang, and along with the dust from previous supernovae explosions, creates a cloud of diffuse gas known as nebula. The nebula fragments into a few collapsing clouds, which in turn forms the next generation of high mass stars: Blue main sequence stars. 10 solar masses, and their surfaces burn brightly as 20 thousand degrees in the ultraviolet, as a sky blue color. The nuclear fusion has restarted once again, this time within the cores of stars!

After another brief 100 million years later, after its formation, the blue main sequence star, runs out of its initial supply of nuclear fuel. It expands from a radius of 5 suns to 530 suns. Its surface temperature drops to 4000 degrees, or about that color of incandescent white light bulb. It is now an orange supergiant star, with its core temperature skyrocketing, inside, it has onion shells of fusing elements and isotopes. It lasts for only another 10 million years.

Every massive orange supergiant star explodes as a type II supernova! This star is no exception. The core has fused its last supply of elements into Iron-56, the most stable nucleus that exists. The core implodes in fractions of a second, into a neutron star remnant. The resulting conversion of nuclei into pure neutrons relases plenty of energy, that ricochets the rest of the stellar matter, creating a massive shockwave that blows it up. The energy from such an explosion outshines the rest of its galaxy for weeks, if not months. The explosion leaves behind a neutron star, which is a dead stellar core of compact neutron matter.

The resulting elements from type II supernovae contains: 23 carbon-12 nuclei, 5 nitrogen-14 nuclei, 156 oxygen-16 nuclei, 14 neon-20 nuclei, 2 neon-22 nuclei, 6 magnesium-24 nuclei, 4 silicon-28 nuclei, 2 sulfur-32 nuclei, and 2 iron-56 nuclei. Massive star explosion, after massive star explosion, the galaxies are being seeded with the elements needed for life itself. Elements like oxygen, which when combined with hydrogen from the big bang, forms lots and lots of water, the universal solvent. The gases and dust grains disperse into the interstellar medium.

A neutron star remnant is a very amazing object. A dense quantum object made of a plasma of only neutrons. However the neutrons are as compact as they can be. This is the same force that prevents solid matter from passing through itself, but here, it applies to the whole neutron star remnant at once: An atomic nucleus as massive as a star. For some facts, a neutron star remnant, has the mass of up to 3 suns, and a radius of a large city.

It is now almost 2 billion years after the big bang. We zoom back out to the intergalactic scale. A billion years have elapsed since the first irregular galaxies formed. As the invisible matter of the cosmic web locks together, two sheets of this invisible matter, with each sheet being a network containing more than a few dozen irregular galaxies, merge together. What was two galaxy clusters is now a grouping of a few larger galactic mergers. Gas, stars, dust, are all flung in all directions. Gravity brings the matter back together into three large spiral disk galaxies, which in turn ignites more stars within these new spiral galaxies. This creates their spiral arms and pinwheel character. In the mean time, the invisible cosmic web has grown so much by now, that what was a network of irregular galaxies is now three huge hubs of the invisible matter, inside each a spiral galaxy. It is as if a cosmic dance is taking place, with the skaters replaced by huge incandescent galaxies. The three new spiral galaxies continue to absorb more and more irregular galaxies into their spiral arms. This slightly disrupts their spiral arms, but after another few billion years, each of these spiral galaxies has grown considerably, now as three grand pinwheels.

Zooming inside one of these spiral galaxies, there is still lots of gas from the big bang, and along with even more of the dust from previous supernovae explosions, which creates several more clouds of diffuse gases that we know as nebulae. These nebulae fragment more, and due to extra turbulence from supernova explosions in nearby spiral arms, they collapse and fragment further into a few hundred collapsing clouds, which in turn forms the next generation of intermediate mass stars. One of these stars is a sun-like star! This star, and all the others in the loosely bound cluster, known as an open star cluster, burn with a surface temperature of 6 thousand degrees, which looks more or less, like white daylight. The open star cluster disapates its sun-like stars into the disk of the spiral galaxy that birthed them. Nuclear fusion has it’s torch passed down to the next generation of stars!

Then, 5 billion years after its formation, this sun-like star expands to 60 times its original radius. It is now a red giant star, and has a surface temperature of 3000 degrees, about that color of an older incandescent light bulb. The star’s core has begun to fuse its helium-4 ash into carbon-12 and oxygen-16. This bloated angry red giant star, lasts for another 1 billion years, until it’s core becomes dangerously unstable. The core gets denser and energy waves from it, expelling plenty of stellar matter away from the diffusing red giant star into what is known as a planetary nebula. After a few thousand years later, what is left over is a very dense dead stellar core of compact atomic matter known as a white dwarf.

The resulting planetary nebula contains: 69 carbon-12 nuclei, and 12 nitrogen-14 nuclei. Like the other supernovae explosions, the planetary nebulae from countless red giant stars seed the galaxies with even more matter, necessary for life, which includes a plenty surplus of carbon. The gases and dust grains disperse into the interstellar medium.

A white dwarf remnant is a quite amazing object. A dense quantum object made of a plasma of nuclei and electrons. However the electrons are as compact as they can be. This is the same force that prevents solid matter from passing through itself, but here, it applies to the whole white dwarf remnant at once: An atom as massive as a star. For some facts, a white dwarf remnant, has the mass of up to 1.4 suns, and a radius of a planet or moon.

Since many sun like stars can come from the same fragmenting nebula, it is very common for this generation of stars to orbit in pairs, and rarely triplets. This allows for a new phenomenon to occur. With an orbiting pair of sun like stars, one might form a planetary nebula first, before the other star even expands out to become a red giant. When the red giant’s atmosphere touches the other white dwarf remnant, it captures lots of gas and dust. The nuclei slam onto its surface, and sometimes can temporarily cause nuclear explosions called novae. However, once the white dwarf remant starts to fuse elements into Iron-56, the same element that kills massive stars one by one, the remnant detonates. The resulting shockwave shreds the other star down to a cloud of plasma. This is called a type Ia supernova. The energy from such a supernova explosion outshines the rest of its galaxy for many weeks. Since every white dwarf remant always reaches the same mass before they explode, each exploding white dwarf remnant always has the same luminosity. A standard candle if you will…

The resulting type Ia supernova contains: 2 silicon-28 nuclei, 1 sulfur-32 nucleus, 1 argon-36 nucleus, and 3 iron-56 nuclei. Although iron as a nuclear fuel, is a star killer, most of the iron in the cosmos comes from supernovae like these. The gases and dust grains disperse into the interstellar medium.

It is 9 billion years since time began. We zoom back out to the intergalactic scale once again. At this point the three main spiral galaxies are about to merge together. In front of us is a barred spiral galaxy about 100 thousand light years across. 2 million light years, over to the left is a grand spiral galaxy, that is 250 thousand light years across. Another 800 thousand light years, further left, is a much smaller pinwheel galaxy, and is only 70 thousand light years across. However, as billions of years in cosmic time pass by, the two main galaxies approach one another, spinning through space, in a knife edge configuration. During their collision, they pass right through each-other, flinging their guts millions of light years into space. Meanwhile, the smaller spiral near the system’s edge, shoots right through the crime scene at a relatively high speed. The seemily double spiral galaxy with two long tails, gradually reforms itself into a new conglomeration of old stars known as an elliptical galaxy. The remants of the two tails continue to get eaten up by the newly formed elliptical galaxy’s immense gravity. The smaller galaxy, comes back into the scene and is absorbed into the new huge galaxy. A slightly flattened eliptical galaxy, known as a lenticular galaxy.

The last stars to form are red dwarf stars: stars smaller than the sun, and far dimmer. Their surface temperature is around 3500 degrees, and are about a quarter of what would be our sun’s mass and radius. They steadily fuse hydrogen-1 into helium-4, over a great timespan. The first of these stars will only fade after many trillions of years into the future, too far into the future as of now. Most red dwarf stars, come in pairs, triplets, or even larger solar systems. Again this is due to the fragmenting nebulae. With much more dust, and plenty of turbulence, from previous supernovae, later and later generations of stars, will tend to be smaller, and more numerous.

This whole process of gravity shaping the cosmic hiearchy, is what makes the universe such an interesting place. The cosmic web expands outwards with its filaments and knots growing. Within the web’s densest interconnections, we have interacting galaxies, with each galaxy an orbiting collection of stars, gas, and black holes. Galaxies merging and evolving from small irregular lumps to great spirals and pinwheels, and lastly to huge diffuse spheres of old red dwarf stars. Deeper still, entire generations of stars have come and gone, spreading the raw ingredients for new solar systems to form later on. And in the process, we might figure out how our universe works!

Chapter 8: Concluding Remarks

We have seen the universe emerge from a theory of everything. Everything from new JWST and HST measurements of the deep sky galaxies, being seeded from primordial galactic mass black holes, to the adherence to the standard model physics, all stems from the use of the best current theories in physics. Organized by the CGH physics cube, and synthesized into the simplest theory of everything by my black hole - particle duality hypothesis. But if such a canidate theory explains everything with no more assumptions, and then gives predictions already found, the black hole duality must be confirmed.

Going through extreme scales, we found boundaries with units. Those units were actually three new laws of physics, that we unified into a picture of a single particle that we called CGH physics. We assert our black hole idea, build a theory compatible with all known physics, and look at its predictions. Now we know it is correct. Thank you Occam’s razor, for making this work. Sometimes if you find beauty in the symmetries of the cosmos, and this time it is in the simplicity and universality of the black hole particle duality.

Given that the theory of everything exists and is in our posession, lets now plug it into a computer, and see how it fairs…
